          +dio/music, and non-simulation UI rolls — cases where randomne
          +ss can never desync gameplay, AI, saves, or deterministic tes
          +ts. **Forbidden** in geometry, generation, AI, loot, spawns, 
          +and any save- or test-affecting logic; use `rng()` / `seededR
          +andom` there. This is the FX/UI carve-out — note the `rng()` 
          +bullet above says "FX, and UI rolls" for the deterministic pa
          +th, but purely-cosmetic FX/UI may instead use `mathRng`.     
      296  - `_overrideRng(fn)` / `_restoreRng()` — **test-only** hooks 
           for mocking. Never use in production code.  
      297  
      297 -The only permitted exception to the `Math.random()` ban: cryp
          -tographic or network-identity values where determinism would 
          -be a security/collision risk (e.g., session tokens, online pe
          -er IDs in `online_client.ts` / `net_sphere.ts`). Such cases m
          -ust be explicitly commented with the reason.                 
      298 +The only permitted exception for **raw** `Math.random()` (i.e
          +. bypassing even the `mathRng` wrapper): cryptographic or net
          +work-identity values where determinism would be a security/co
          +llision risk (e.g., session tokens, online peer IDs in `onlin
          +e_client.ts` / `net_sphere.ts`). Such cases must be explicitl
          +y commented with the reason.                                 
      299  
      300  In tests, do not mock `Math.random`. Use `_overrideRng(() =>
           value)` / `_restoreRng()` or `seedGlobalRng(seed)`.
      301  

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ This is actually a positive headline with a minor footnote:
  MonsterKind.TUMANNIK is the only content-specific monster check in all 3800+
  lines of webgl.ts — the renderer is otherwise exemplary at the "reads state,
  draws" contract. And even this one check is redundant: fogOffsetX/Y is set
  exclusively by Tumannik AI (monster.ts:7826), so any entity with a non-zero
  offset is a Tumannik by construction — the kind-guard could be dropped with
  identical behavior. The renderer does not own gameplay state here (AI
  computes the offset; renderer only reads it), so it's not the true
  anti-pattern — just a mild redundant coupling. Let me record it
  proportionately as C14 and verify world.ts/webgl.ts cleanliness is worth
  stating positively.

⏺ I found something worth recording. The invariant holds by value
  (SURFACE_MAP_MAX_CELLS = 1024 == SURF_MAX_SLOTS = 1024) — good. But there are
  two subtle issues in this WIP:

  1. The last hunk is suspicious. The diff tail shows + if (!cell) cell = 
  acquireSurfaceCell(world, ci); — but acquireSurfaceCell already does the
  get-or-allocate. So this call site does world.surfaceMap.get(ci) → then if 
  (!cell) acquireSurfaceCell which gets again. Harmless (double-get), but it
  means that call site's local cell was fetched some other way first. Let me
  read it — it could be a logic seam.
  2. Cap can be exceeded when all cells are functional (comment acknowledges),
  and SURFACE_MAP_MAX_CELLS == SURF_MAX_SLOTS exactly (not <). If the map
  exceeds 1024 (all-functional edge), the renderer at webgl.ts:3003/3483 takes
  the expensive > SURF_MAX_SLOTS path — the exact decay the change prevents.
  The comment says "≤" but the values are "==", leaving zero headroom.
  
  Let me read the ambiguous call site and confirm before recording.
  
⏺ Bash(cd /Users/jirnyak/Mirror/gigahrush
      echo "=== the 'if (!cell) cell = acquireSurfaceCell' call site — full
      context ==="…)
  ⎿  === the 'if (!cell) cell = acquireSurfaceCell' call site — full conte
     xt ===
       let cell = world.surfaceMap.get(ci);
     … +17 lines (ctrl+o to expand)
  ⎿  Allowed by auto mode classifier
     
⏺ This call site is actually correct and clever — a lazy-allocation
  optimization: it pre-fetches cell once, then only calls acquireSurfaceCell
  (which allocates + counts against the cap) if a pixel actually passes the
  alpha/intensity threshold. This avoids allocating a 1KB tile for a stamp that
  writes nothing. Good WIP, not a bug.

  So the only substantive note is the exact == cap headroom — and even that is
  defensible (functional cells are "chalk-map clues, craft/interactive
  fixtures", realistically few, so exceeding 1024 all-functional is
  near-impossible in practice). This is a strengthening of B0, not a
  regression: the owner's WIP fixes a real perf-decay driver correctly. Let me
  update B0 to reflect the grown WIP set honestly and record this positively.

⏺ Confirmed: MAX_DRAW = 40 → MAX_DIST = 40.0, MAX_STEPS = 80, SKY_STEPS = 24.

  Now the first-principles march math. Rays are unit vectors, so ddx = 
  1/|rayDX|, ddy = 1/|rayDY| are the parametric (t) distances between grid
  lines. dEnter grows fastest per step for an axis-aligned ray (one step ≈ +1.0
  t); any diagonal crosses more grid lines per unit t, so grows slower. Upper
  bound after 24 steps ≈ **t=24 < 40**. Therefore dEnter > MAX_DIST (line 1448)
  can **never** fire within the 24-step cap → skyEscape stays false → the
  sky-terminal draw at 1462–1466 is unreachable. The subagent's core math is
  correct.
  
  Now I must verify the two things I haven't personally confirmed: (a) what
  actually renders in the loop-exhaustion fall-through (to state the visual bug
  precisely, not overstate it), and (b) the git regression at line 1540.
  
  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Verified from first principles: with unit-length rays, dEnter advances by at
  most ~1.0 in t per step (axis-aligned worst case; diagonals slower), so after
  SKY_STEPS=24 iterations dEnter ≤ ~24 < MAX_DIST=40. The guard if (dEnter > 
  MAX_DIST) skyEscape = true (1448) can never fire, and 1448 is the only write
  to skyEscape (declared false at 1419). Hence the dynamic-sky terminal draw at
  1462–1466 is unreachable. Core math confirmed.

  Now the git regression claim on line 1540. Let me verify the commit exists
  and diff that exact hunk against its parent.

          +ерацию (худший

⏺ The rendering audit is folded in and the audit pass is complete. Final summary of this stretch:

  C16 recorded — HIGH rendering regression (independently verified)

  The last subagent flagged a bug in the see-through-ceilings commit 06349513. Before recording it I re-derived every load-bearing claim from source rather than trusting the report:

  - Dead sky terminal (math, from first principles): SKY_STEPS = 24 (webgl.ts:241) caps the ceiling march, but MAX_DIST = 40 (MAX_DRAW=40, types.ts:5). Unit-length rays advance dEnter by ≤ ~1.0 t/step, so after 24 steps dEnter ≤ ~24 < 40 — the
  only writer of skyEscape (:1448 if (dEnter > MAX_DIST)) never fires, making the dynamic-sky draw at :1459-1466 unreachable dead code.
  - Git regression (proven vs 06349513^): the parent had a bare if (uUseDynamicSky == 1) sky-ceiling gate and no see-through march at all; the commit narrowed it to && uHasOpenSky == 0 (:1540), disabling the old sky path on open-sky floors while
  its intended replacement is unreachable.
  - Scope: hasOpenSky: true is set on exactly two floors — roof (z+50) and outer_district (z+48) — the only ones with sky providers. On both, dynamic sky (time-of-day/sun/clouds) renders as flat fog + a concrete lid.

  Reconciliation

  - C3 re-graded from "✅ чисто / no regressions" → "⚠️  сужено": its two actually-checked aspects (two-renderer sync, SKY_TIER threshold) were re-confirmed clean; the blanket verdict overreached because it never examined march reachability. Kept
  its valid content, redirected the conclusion to C16.
  - Section D updated: D.1.4 (visual-work praise now carries the C16 caveat) and D.2.1 (roof is both the endgame destination and a broken open-sky floor — double hit to the climax).
  - Summary table: C3 row updated, C16 row + a verified severity tally added (1 BLOCKER, 6 HIGH, 2 MED).

  Integrity

  No code touched (webgl.ts is RED + the hard "ТЫ НЕ МЕНЯЕШЬ КОД" constraint). git diff --check clean; my only edits are audit.md (new) and items.md:11/52 (doc-drift counts). All 24 dirty .ts files remain untouched owner WIP.

  All seven audit passes (save/load, combat/PSI, quests, economy, samosbor/A-Life, rendering, plus docs+WIP) are complete and cross-referenced in audit.md — 16 subsystem findings (C1–C16) plus A/B doc-and-WIP findings.

  C16 recorded — HIGH rendering regression (independently verified)

  The last subagent flagged a bug in the see-through-ceilings commit 06349513. Before recording it I re-derived every load-bearing claim from source rather than trusting the report:

  - Dead sky terminal (math, from first principles): SKY_STEPS = 24 (webgl.ts:241) caps the ceiling march, but MAX_DIST = 40 (MAX_DRAW=40, types.ts:5). Unit-length rays advance dEnter by ≤ ~1.0 t/step, so after 24 steps dEnter ≤ ~24 < 40 — the
  only writer of skyEscape (:1448 if (dEnter > MAX_DIST)) never fires, making the dynamic-sky draw at :1459-1466 unreachable dead code.
  - Git regression (proven vs 06349513^): the parent had a bare if (uUseDynamicSky == 1) sky-ceiling gate and no see-through march at all; the commit narrowed it to && uHasOpenSky == 0 (:1540), disabling the old sky path on open-sky floors while
  its intended replacement is unreachable.
  - Scope: hasOpenSky: true is set on exactly two floors — roof (z+50) and outer_district (z+48) — the only ones with sky providers. On both, dynamic sky (time-of-day/sun/clouds) renders as flat fog + a concrete lid.
